# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2021-07-01_TotalExtraction_82803916.pdf
   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 28, 2021 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  
Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 14, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –EMERGENCY READING – Amending Section 10.80, Bicycles, of Title 
10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming (Sponsor – Mr. Segrave). 
Mayor Collins advised that the City Attorney reported a large vendor of electronic scooters (e-
scooters) has plans to locate here beginning in July and has requested emergency approval of 3 
ordinances in order to provide for appropriate licensing and enforcement regulations prior to their 
arrival.  Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, explained there are 3 proposed ordinances related to 
e-scooter and e-bicycle legislation.  He noted there are currently e-scooters operating in the city 
and it is anticipated there will be more companies coming to Cheyenne.  Mr. O’Donnell advised 
the ordinances focus on public safety particularly noting the upcoming Cheyenne Frontier Days 
(CFD) event.  Upon request, Mr. O’Donnell reported on this agenda item and the 2 affiliated 
ordinances and advised this ordinance amends City Code Section 10.80 and is a modification of 
the existing bicycle ordinance and includes definitions for e-scooters and related devices.  He 
advised Chapter 10.82 creates a regulatory structure for the devices that are for hire only, noting it 
specifically excludes the DDA Re-Ride Program.  He further reported it sets up the structure for a 
business license and fees, a series of requirements for how the business shall be run, insurance and 
indemnification requirements, suspension and revocation provisions that are established within the 
City Code administrative hearing process and public education required.  He noted enforcement 
occurs through the Police Department, City Clerk, Compliance Division and occasionally through 
the Planning & Development Department.  It was further advised there are anticipated amendments 
to Chapter 10.82.  Mr. O’Donnell provided highlights of the proposed Indemnification Agreement 
which was reviewed by Bird Rides, Inc., a large operator planning to operate in Cheyenne, noting 
there are significant insurance and indemnification requirements.  Mr. O’Donnell explained 
amendments to Chapter 5.04 include adding the license in the business license section of the City 

   
Code.  Mayor Collins provided information relating to approval on Emergency Reading and 
explained a motion to suspend the rules is required, noting a three-quarters affirmative vote (8 out 
of 10 members of the Governing Body) is required to proceed for emergency approval.  Mayor 
Collins advised if the motion to suspend the rules fails, the item would be referred to the Finance 
Committee for a recommendation.  Mr. White moved to suspend the rules and hear this ordinance 
as an emergency ordinance pursuant to Wyoming State Statute 15-1-115(c), seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  During public comment, Jeremiah Mahoney spoke in support of approving regulations 
prior to an influx of scooters being utilized by citizens.  Michael Covato, Bird Rides, Inc., Territory 
Manager, expressed enthusiasm for the opportunity to locate in Cheyenne.  Upon inquiry, Mr. 
Covato advised the company has been internally assessing operations in Cheyenne over the last 
year and did outreach earlier this year, however formal conversations began several weeks ago.  It 
was noted from members of the Governing Body that although there is excitement for the 
operation, the emergency ordinance regulatory process seems rushed and additional time to 
research and make appropriate decisions is preferred.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Covato provided 
operations expectations and information relating to CFD events, seasonal operations, and local 
management.  Mr. Covato expressed the desire to partner with local members of the community 
to hire a local Fleet Manager.  Comments were made related to time frame for scooters to arrive 
and time necessary to prepare required licensing and insurance provisions.  Upon further inquiry, 
Mr. Covato provided information on the number of e-scooters the company plans to start with 
locally which will be monitored for potential increases.  It was advised that geofencing regulations 
and limitations would be enforced, noting changing the area of the geofence is a simple process.  
Mr. O’Donnell noted the anticipated amendment defines geofencing that allows for flexibility at 
the discretion of the director, noting the director will work with companies to determine what those 
requirements should be.  Hamilton Byrd, local concert promoter, reported he and his partner have 
recently launched Bandwagon e-scooters in the community and they have been working with the 
Downtown Development Authority (DDA) and the Metropolitan Planning Organization (MPO) 
for several months.  Mr. Byrd reported the top speed of a scooter is limited to 20 kilometers per 
hour, helmets are suggested and currently they have 32 e-scooters in operation.  He advised the 
MPO provided them with suggested geofencing requirements and noted the age minimum is 16 
for operation.  He further advised upon inquiry, that he supports regulations and will work with 
the city to become compliant and confirmed alteration of geofencing is easily obtained.  Jenny 
Clough expressed safety concerns for riders and pedestrians related to street and sidewalk 
conditions.  Ms. Clough voiced support of e-scooters but cautioned operations during CFD.   
Motion (to suspend the rules) carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  City Clerk Kris Jones read the ordinance entitlement into 
record upon request of Mayor Collins.  Upon inquiry, City Clerk Kris Jones explained that under 
the emergency ordinance procedure, a three-quarters affirmative vote (8 out of 10 members of the 
Governing Body) in order for the ordinance to pass.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by 
Mr. White.  It was noted by members of the governing body that the issues brought up by Ms. 
Clough demonstrates the need for requirements to be addressed immediately and that other cities 
have had to ban scooters as businesses came in without regulations in place.  Additional comments 
were made in support of efforts by the attorney’s office and support was voiced for the e-scooters 
but concern was expressed related to the fast paced approval of the ordinances.  Mayor Collins 
noted occasionally the need arises for approval of emergency procedures.    Upon inquiry, Mr. 
O’Donnell reported an ordinance exists that addresses skateboards in the Central Business District 

   
(CBD).  It was noted that in the future electric unicycles will be considered for inclusion of 
regulations set forth within the affiliated ordinances.  Mayor Collins and Mr. O’Donnell confirmed 
that these vehicles would be allowed to operate on the greenway as they are categorized by state 
statute as non-motorized due to engine size. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  (#4378)  
 
 
ORDINANCE –EMERGENCY READING – Creating a new Chapter 10.82, Motor-
Assisted Scooters, Electric Bicycles, and Bicycles Equipped with GPS, of Title 10, Vehicles and 
Traffic, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming (Sponsor – Mr. Segrave).  Mr.  White 
moved to suspend the rules and hear this ordinance as an emergency ordinance pursuant to 
Wyoming State Statute 15-1-115(c), seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. O’Donnell read into record the following 
proposed amendments:  Section 10.82.020-Definitions and Exclusions-addition of “Geofencing” 
means the use of a virtual perimeter in which the use of a dockless vehicle is either restricted or 
the speed of the dockless vehicle is reduced when in use; 10.82.050-Dockless Vehicle Operations-
F-Notices of violations or broken or inoperable dockless vehicles: 1.  License holders shall have 
two hours after receiving notice of a violation, and 10.85.050(I):  License holders shall adhere to 
geofencing requirements, as determined and at the discretion of the director.  Mr. Segrave moved 
to amend by referenced language changes provided by Mr. O’Donnell, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  
Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell provided enforcement processes and confirmed the city would work 
within the spirit of the law for enforcement efforts.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” - all 
members of the governing body.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to July 12, 2021, seconded by Mr. 
Cook.  Support and opposition was expressed for the proposed postponement.  Jillian Harris, 
Transportation Planner for MPO, provided details on the history of efforts to implement legislation 
for regulations which began in February 2021.  Ms. Harris advised that introduction of e-scooters 
was inevitable and that the MPO has taken necessary steps to work with city staff and e-scooter 
operators to protect the citizens.  Ms. Harris explained the MPO had been working with the 
Bandwagon providers and Bird Rides, Inc. contacted the MPO in March and presented a 
memorandum of understanding (MOU) which was catalyst to move the legislation as quickly as 
possible in the best interest of the community from a public safety standpoint.  Michael Covato, 
Bird Rides, Inc., advised there are planning logistics that can’t be done until an ordinance outlining 
regulations is in effect, voicing opposition to the postponement.  Kris Jones, City Clerk, upon 
inquiry, advised staff has been working on ordinance details including the business license 
component, noting final document details will require additional adjustments pushing 
implementation further out if postponed.  Mr. O’Donnell shared that coordination with insurance 
companies requires time to assure all requirements are in place, noting that a delay would most 
likely result in missing the timeline of being ready prior to CFD.  Mr. Johnson noted for the record 
he opposes rushed approval which denies the public opportunity to participate.  Mr. O’Donnell 
reported future ordinance amendments can take place after approval through the regular council 
approval process, noting the importance of having regulations in place prior to implementation of 
e-scooters in the community.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” - Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson 
and Dr. Rinne.  Voting “no” - Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. 
Roybal, Mr. Segrave and Mr. White.  Mr. O’Donnell reported that the Indemnification and 
Insurance provisions will be finalized by staff, can be adjusted when necessary, and are not a part 

   
of the ordinance but are referenced in the ordinance.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by capping the 
number of e-scooters to a total of 80, seconded by seconded by Mr. Cook.  It was noted that 
Bandwagon already has 32 e-scooters active and that limiting the amount could raise legal concern 
with regard to restraint of trade.  Comments were made in support and in opposition of the 
proposed amendment, and support was voiced for a free market approach.  Mr. O’Donnell voiced 
concern with a first come first serve environment limiting those who can apply for a license to 
operate.  Tom Mason, MPO Director, advised that without rules and regulations in place, the 
program should be restricted to a pilot program and that he anticipates use of e-scooters as mode 
of transportation, noting 80 scooters isn’t sufficient for that purpose.  Additional comments were 
made in support of letting the marketplace and company expertise determine the number of e-
scooters the community needs.  Boyd Wiggam voiced opposition to capping the e-scooter amount 
and cautioned against restraint of trade noting he believes a cap would be detrimental in allowing 
this as a transportation type in the community.  He further voiced support of e-scooters in the 
community.  Additional detailed comments were made in support and in opposition of capping 
fleet numbers, free trade/market concept, restraint of trade, public safety concerns and uncharted 
territory relating to e-scooters.   Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting “yes”.   Main motion as 
amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. 
Johnson voting “no”.  (#4379) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –EMERGENCY READING – Amending Section 5.04.030, Types of 
Businesses, Activities and Events, of Chapter 5.04, Business Licenses Generally, and creating a 
new Section 5.08.155, Motor-Assisted Scooters, Electric Bicycles and Bicycles Equipped with 
GPS, of Chapter 5.08, Business License Fees, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne (Sponsor – Mr. Segrave).  Mr. White moved to suspend the rules

115(c), seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mr. Cook and Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#4380)

ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 8.60.210, Liens, of Chapter 8.60, 
Nuisances, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Upon 
inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell provided clarification that the city would take priority of all liens except 
prior tax or assessment liens, noting the purpose is intended to place city liens on high priority.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Supportive 
comments were voiced, and it was noted this action is necessary to act responsibly in certain 
situations the city faces regarding public health and safety issues.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body. 
 
 

   
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 12.04.240, Liens, of Chapter 
12.04, Streets, Sidewalks, and Driveways Generally, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public 
Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Comments were voiced regarding widespread damage and 
issues with streets, sidewalks and driveways that need addressed.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Creating a new Section 15.24.030, Liens, of Chapter 
15.24, Dangerous Building Code, of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell provided information on removal of a dangerous building 
near the municipal building and the city is currently in litigation to recoup the expense of removal.  
He further explained the intent of the proposed lien ordinances are to allow the city to be first in 
line to recoup expenses.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.

of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division (Solid Waste Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(accepted bid from Peterbilt of Wyoming, Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $689,094.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-29-21 for furnishing one (1) new 2021-2022 twin-screw diesel 
powered cab and chassis, for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division (Solid Waste Fund).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Peterbilt of Wyoming, Cheyenne, in an 
amount not to exceed $119,264.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-30-21 for furnishing three (3) new current in production 2021-
2022 solid waste transfer trailers, for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division (Solid Waste 
Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Ameri-Tech Equipment Co., 
Evansville, WY, in an amount not to exceed $311,700.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-31-21 for furnishing one (1) new 2021-2022 front end wheel 
loader with less than 10 hours (Transfer Station), for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division 
(Solid Waste Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Honnen Equipment, 
Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $172,400.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-32-21 for furnishing one (1) new 2021-2022 front end wheel 
loader with less than 10 hours (Compost Facility), for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation Division 
(Solid Waste Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Honnen Equipment, 
Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $206,000.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-33-21 for furnishing one (1) new 2021-2022 portable trommel 
compost, topsoil screen plant, for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation/Compost Division (Solid Waste 
Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Argus Industrial Co., Pontiac, 
MI, in an amount not to exceed $131,971.67). 
 

   
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-34-21 for furnishing one (1) new 2021-2022 horizontal mobile 
wood waste grinder, for the City of Cheyenne, Sanitation/Compost Division (Solid Waste Fund).  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Rotochopper, Albany, MN, in an amount 
not to exceed $389,707.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-36-21 for furnishing one (1) new 2021-2022 1 ton, regular cab, 
full size pickup truck, with plow, for the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation and Events 
Department (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from 
Spradley Barr Motors, Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $45,585.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-38-21 for furnishing one (1) new 2021-2022 15 passenger van, 
for the City of Cheyenne, Community Recreation and Events Department (2015-2018 1% Sales 
Tax and Insurance Claims).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Greiner 
Ford, Casper, WY, in an amount not to exceed $33,270.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration to purchase between the City of Cheyenne, and CenturyLink, Littleton, CO, 
to relocate CenturyLink owned fiber optic utilities in preparation for an upcoming construction 
project (Specific Purpose Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to 
exceed $72,000.00). 
 
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 
No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
 
Reappointments of Robert Korber to the Laramie County Community Juvenile Services 
Joint Powers Board and Patrick Brady to the Community Action of Laramie County Board of 
Directors.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote 
(mayoral appointments).   
 
 
Appointments of Kim Skoutary-Johnson to the Laramie County Juvenile Services Board; 
Stephanie Reece and Wendy Volk to the Downtown Development Authority Board; Stephanie 
Reece to the Visit Cheyenne Board; Trey Rinne and Kevin Marquardt to the Cheyenne/Laramie 
County Economic Development Board; Curtis Clabaugh and Milo Vigil to the Board of 
Adjustment and Lauren Johnson, Carol Behrend, Samantha Cook, and Donald Potter to the 
Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities. Dr. Rinne declared conflict of interest due to a 
proposed appointment of a family member and did not participate in discussion or vote.  Mr. 
Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins (mayoral appointments) and Dr. Rinne 
who did not vote.   
 
[CA] 
Designation of Official Voting Delegate and Alternate Delegate for the City of Cheyenne 
at the July 15, 2021 Wyoming Association of Municipalities Summer Convention Business 
Meeting to be held in Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (appointed Dr. 
Mark Rinne as Official Voting Delegate and Mayor Patrick Collins as Alternate Voting Delegate). 

   
 
Announcement was made that a Calcutta benefitting Cheyenne area charities, will be held 
Sunday, July 31, 2021 beginning at 5:30 p.m. in the Cowboy Bistro located beside the Rodeo 
Committee Cabin at Frontier Park, Cheyenne, Wyoming, in conjunction with the 2021 Cheyenne 
Frontier Days Rodeo.  (10% of proceeds go to bona fide charities).   
 
 
Announcement was made that due to the Independence Day holiday on July 4, 2021 and 
City offices being closed on Monday, July 5, 2021, regularly scheduled City Council Committee 
meetings will instead be held in the Municipal Building, Room 104, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, and via electronic conference meetings as follows:  Finance Committee will 
meet Tuesday, July 6, 2021 at Noon and Public Services Committee will meet Wednesday, July 
7, 2021 Noon.  
 
 
 
Under other business: 
 
 
Mayor Collins expressed appreciation to Community Recreation and Events staff for their 
hard work and efforts relating to the June 26th Superday activities and noted the excellent condition 
of Lions Park.   
 
 
Mr. Laybourn noted he will be sending each member of the Community Recreation & 
Events Department an email expressing appreciation for their efforts and hard work on the annual 
Superday event.  Mayor Collins, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Segrave echoed Mr. Laybourn’ s comments 
and supports a staff email.   
 
 
Dr. Aldrich advised she recently participated in a panel during affordable housing week 
and was impressed by a local contractor and the Planning & Development Department for their 
efforts.  She further noted supportive comments that were received by local area contractors 
related to Mr. Bloom’s leadership of the city’s Planning & Development Department.  She 
further provided information on a kick-off meeting for the new Technical Advisory Council and 
Innovations and Entrepreneurial Task Force that will be held on Tuesday, June 29th, beginning at 
5:30 p.m. at the Array Building.  
 
 
Mr. Johnson commented on a 13-year-old he met during Superday and expressed 
appreciation for his drive and work ethic noting he was raising awareness and funds for his church. 
 
 
  
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 8:10 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
July 9, 2021

63.56 feet more or less to the point of beginning. 
 
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real 
property described herein and to make it a part thereof; and

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-21-00129:  Park Estates, 4th Filing Annexation 
REQUEST: 
An annexation of 1.4 acres of land  
LOCATION: 
Southwest of the intersection of Parkhill, Braehill, and Panorama.  
APPLICANT: 
TL Provision, LLC 
AGENT: 
AVI, PC 
OWNER: 
Darold Stephen Melchior, Et Ux 
PURPOSE: 
To allow urban-level residential development 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00129
May 10, 2021
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing of 1.4 acres of land for future single-unit residential development. The 
County Assessor presently shows the land as a portion of Tract 5, Park Estates and an unplatted parcel  
however, this property is proposed to be replatted as Park Estates, 4th Filing in the County and recorded as 
such prior to the final reading of this annexation ordinance. The proposed parcel would be Tract 2 of the 
proposed Park Estates, 4th Filing plat. The land is currently developed with one single-family home in the 
County. A zone change is proposed concurrently with this annexation. Both applications are anticipated to 
come to final consideration before the Governing Body at the same meeting.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by a submission 
to change the zoning from County AR Agricultural Residential to the City zone of MR Medium-density 
Residential.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned AR Agricultural Residential in the County. The property is concurrently 
proposed to be annexed and rezoned to the MR Medium-density Residential. The MR zone is generally 
described as a primarily residential zone with compact neighborhood development patterns. The district 
generally allows a variety of residential uses with some smaller-scale civic or commercial uses.      
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the occupants of 
future development once it is built. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to

UDC-21-00129
May 10, 2021
City Fire and Police and sanitation services. The property will also be connected to City water 
and sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The contiguity to the east makes the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. 
City-developed residential properties are also in close proximity to the south. The development 
of this property will benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the social and 
economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. City water and sewer lines exist in the vicinity and could be 
extended with the development of the property. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 31.3% contiguous, along the eastern edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 .   
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 

UDC-21-00129
May 10, 2021
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on April 8, 2021. The public 
hearing for this annexation is scheduled for May 10, 2021. Twenty (20) business days prior to 
the announced public hearing is April 12, 2021. The earliest confirmation of receipt was dated 
April 9, 2021. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne anticipates this area of the community to develop with an 
urban residential style of development. These areas are expected to develop with full access to urban 
services  such areas are best served by being within City limits. Policies in PlanCheyenne support 
development within the Urban Service Boundary and the utilization of existing infrastructure.  
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were made noting the need to extend City services.    
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
services typically available to urban areas are available to this property.   
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property.  
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Aerial, Current City Limits, and Future Land Use Map 
2. Submitted Application 
3. Applicant-submitted annexation map

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM 
COUNTY AR AGRICULTURAL RESIDENTIAL TO MR MEDIUM-DENSITY 
RESIDENTIAL FOR LAND LOCATED SOUTHWEST OF THE INTERSECTION 
OF BRAEHILL ROAD, PARKHILL ROAD, AND PANORAMA DRIVE.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment,
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
Section 2. That on May 3, 2021, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change. 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as Tract 1, Park Estates, 4th
Filing is hereby changed to MR Medium-density Residential. 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.

A request to rezone approximately 1.1 acres of land from County AR 
Agricultural Residential to MR Medium-density Residential in concert 
with an annexation

May 3, 2021
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Tract 1, Park Estates, 4th Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming from County 
AR Agricultural Residential to MR Medium-density Residential as shown in the attached zone change 
map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County AR Agricultural Residential to MR 
Medium-density Residential for 1.1 acres of land in concert with an annexation. The land is anticipated to 
developed as a single-unit residential uses, with associated open areas. The land is currently occupied by 
one single-family home. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary and existing 
infrastructure exists in the neighborhood to the southeast. Development will require 
infrastructure extensions and improvements.   
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Urban Residential. The optimal zoning for 
the proposed future land use designation is any of the residential zones, including the MR zone. 
 
 
Future Land Use Designation

UDC-21-00131
May 3, 2021
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1.
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
The existing zoning to the south, north, and west is AR and outside of City limits. The property to 
the east is zoned MR Medium-density Residential within City limits. The future land uses 
anticipated by the Comprehensive Plan and other official plans for this area anticipate higher-
intensity, urban-style residential uses, and the MR zone is both compatible with existing zoning 
in the area and with the future land use designation.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current County AR zone allows limited development options and is intended for low-
intensity rural uses. Generally, parcels in this zone district are not expected to connect to urban 
services. The proposed MR zone allows a variety of more urban development options. Also, as 
the property is being annexed into the City, a City zone is required. 
   
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The standards of Article 4 apply to MR zoning; however, due to the limited scope of this zone 
change area, the existing development patterns, and the topographic constraints, there is 
limited opportunities to apply the standards of Article 4 to the development of this site. 
Required open space and infrastructure improvements would be required with subsequent 
development actions.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The MR zone is intended to develop with a variety of urban-style residential uses. The MR zone 
is applicable to areas designated Urban Residential by the Future Land Use Map, and is intended 
to be within ½ mile of commercial centers. This property is anticipated to develop with urban-
style uses in the future, and is within ½ mile of the commercial center located along Dell Range 
near College. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    

UDC-21-00131
May 3, 2021
6)
The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The applicant is seeking the proposed zone change in concert with an annexation. The proposed 
MR zone is the zoning of the surrounding properties within City limits. A specific development 
pattern is proposed with this application, but should that application not be executed, the MR 
zoning provides a variety of development options compatible with the surrounding 
neighborhoods. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
The application conforms to PlanCheyenne per the discussion with Review Criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no issues were identified by staff, and no comments regarding the 
proposed zone change were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The zoning map 
amendment application is proposed in concert with an annexation, and the MR zone is the City zone 
contiguous to the subject property. The proposed MR zone is also consistent with the future 
development patterns associated with the future land use designation. Staff recommends approval of 
the zoning map amendment as submitted by the applicant. 
 
 
 

UDC-21-00131
May 3, 2021
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation). 
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications. 
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County AR to MR.

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, LAND LOCATED NORTHEAST OF AND ADJACENT TO 
THE INTERSECTION OF DELL RANGE BOULEVARD AND WHITNEY 
ROAD.” 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.
That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows: 
(a) 
That PKT Development, LLC, owners of:
A parcel of land located in the southwest one-quarter of Section 24, Township 14 
North Range 66 West of the 6th Principal Meridian; being more particularly 
described as follows:
 
Commencing at the City of Cheyenne control point “Dell Range” thence 
N74°53'24”E a distance of 874.14 feet to a point on the northerly right of way line 
of Dell Range Boulevard being the true point of beginning, thence along north 
right of way line of Dell Range Boulevard N89°44'57”W a distance of 1748.53 
feet to a point; thence N00°26'28”E a distance of 865.33 feet to a point; thence 
S56°16'47”E a distance of 187.03 feet to a point; thence S56°14'01”E a distance 
of 252.18 feet to a point; thence S56°12'59”E a distance of 295.28 feet to a point; 
thence S56°13'08”E a distance of 303.74 feet to a point; thence S89°50'57”E a 
distance of 312.23 to a point; thence S89°52'00”E a distance of 318.17 feet to a 
point; thence S60°00'18”E a distance of 345.22 feet; thence S22°34'36"W 
distance of 131.84 to the point of beginning. 
 
have petitioned the governing body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real 
property described herein and to make it a part thereof; and  
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as 
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Adam E. Deschler, a Professional Land 
Surveyor, licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 12045; and 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, 
be furnished; and 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned MU 
Mixed Use and LR Low Density Residential by the Laramie County 
Commissioners; and 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and 
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area 
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the 
entire annexed area. 
 
Section 2.
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
Section 3.
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 

Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to 
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4. 
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared 
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the 
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
Section 5. 
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-21-00197:  Annexation of Meadowlark Estates, 2nd Filing  
REQUEST: 
An annexation of 17.8 acres of land (21.8 if Dell Range right-of-way is included)
LOCATION: 
Northeast of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and 
Whitney Road.  
APPLICANT: 
PKT Development LLC  Butch Tilton 
AGENT: 
Bruce Perryman, AVI, PC  
OWNER: 
Meadowlark Estates LLC, c/o PKT Development LLC 
PURPOSE: 
To allow future urban-level development 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Planner II  Development 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00197
June 14, 2021
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
East Dell Range Blvd / US 30 Corridor Study 
 
Whitney Road Corridor Plan 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing of 17.8 acres of land for future residential and commercial 
development. The County Assessor presently shows the tract of land as part of one larger unplatted parcel. 
The land is currently undeveloped in the County. A zone change is proposed concurrently with this 
annexation. Both applications are anticipated to come to final consideration before the Governing Body at 
the same meeting.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by a submission 
to change the zoning from County MU Mixed-Use and County LR Low-density Residential to the City zones 
of CB Community Business, LR Low-density Residential, and MR Medium-density Residential. The purpose of 
this annexation is to allow future urban development un
. A preliminary plat for the 
area, with lots and blocks designed for residential and commercial development, has also been submitted. It 
is anticipated that a final plat would come before the Governing Body within a year of this annexation. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned MU Mixed-Use and LR Low-density Residential in the County. The 
property is concurrently proposed to be annexed and rezoned to the CB Community Business, LR Low-
density Residential, and MR Medium-density Residential zones. The CB zone is intended for retail, 
employment, service and civic uses that may serve a number of residential neighborhoods. Typically, 
areas zoned CB are expected to be more automobile-oriented and serve the region. Commercial 
development at this intersection is anticipated by the properties mixed-use future land use designation, 
and any commercial development at this intersection would likely have a regional service area (including 
the surrounding rural and low-to-medium density residential developments). Both the LR and MR zones 
are intended for residential development with access to public infrastructure and city services. Low or 
medium density residential development is appropriate at this location.

UDC-21-00197
June 14, 2021
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the occupants of 
future development once it is built. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to 
City Fire and Police and sanitation services. The property will also be connected to City water 
and sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The contiguity to the west and the proximity to urban-style development to the 
south makes the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The development of this property 
will benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the social and economic fabric 
that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. City water and sewer lines exist in the vicinity and could be 
extended with the development of the property (the preliminary plat associated with this 
annexation shows utility extensions). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation are about 18.2% contiguous, along the western edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
 
 
 

UDC-21-00197
June 14, 2021
5)
If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on May 14, 2021. The public 
hearing for this annexation is scheduled for June 14, 2021. Twenty (20) business days prior to 
the announced public hearing is May 14, 2021. The earliest confirmation of delivery was dated 
May 17, 2021. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
East Dell Range Blvd / US 30 Corridor Study 
 
Whitney Road Corridor Plan 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with a mix of commercial and lower-density urban residential styles of 
development. These areas are expected to develop with full access to urban services  such areas are 
best served by being within City limits. Policies in PlanCheyenne support development within the Urban 
Service Boundary and the utilization of existing infrastructure.  
 
The East Dell Range Blvd / US 30 Corridor Study and the Whitney Road Corridor Plan both anticipate that 
this area would develop with urban-style commercial and residential uses. Urban-style uses are best 
served by being within City limits and connecting to City services. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, the following comments were received: 
- 
Annexation should bring adjacent Dell Range Boulevard into city limits 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
services typically available to urban areas are available to this property.   

UDC-21-00197
June 14, 2021
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. Staff recommends the inclusion of the 
adjacent portion of Dell Range right-of-way in the annexation.

3. Applicant-submitted annexation map

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM 
COUNTY MU MIXED USE AND COUNTY LR LOW DENSITY RESIDENTIAL 
TO CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS, LR LOW-DENSITY RESIDENTIAL, AND MR 
MEDIUM-DENSITY RESIDENTIAL FOR LAND  LOCATED NORTHEAST OF 
AND ADJACENT TO THE INTERSECTION OF DELL RANGE BOULEVARD
AND WHITNEY ROAD.” 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein have applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment,
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
Section 2. That on June 7, 2021, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change. 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as Lot 1, Block 1, 
Meadowlark Estates, 2nd Filing is hereby changed to CB Community Business; the zoning classification 
of the real property described as Lots 2-7, Block 1 and all of Block 2, Meadowlark Estates, 2nd Filing is 
hereby changed to LR Low-density Residential; and the zoning classification of the real property described 
as all of Block 3, Meadowlark Estates, 2nd Filing is hereby changed to MR Medium-density Residential. 
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below. 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication, 
and upon the official filing of the affiliated Meadowlark Estates, 2nd Filing final plat map with the County 
Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.

UDC-21-00198
June 7, 2021
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lot 1, Block 1, Meadowlark Estates, 2nd Filing to CB Community 
Business; Lots 2-7, Block 1 and all of Block 2, Meadowlark Estates, 2nd Filing to LR Low-density 
Residential; and all of Block 3, Meadowlark Estates, 2nd Filing to MR Medium-density Residential as 
shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County MU Mixed-Use and County LR 
Low-density Residential to CB Community Business, LR Low-density Residential, and MR Medium-
density Residential for 17 acres of vacant land in concert with an annexation. The land is anticipated to be 
developed in the future with commercial and residential style uses and associated streets.    
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary. Infrastructure does 
not currently exist in the area and development will require infrastructure extensions and 
improvements; however, adjacent developments may assist in the extension of infrastructure.

UDC-21-00198
June 7, 2021
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Mixed-use Commercial Emphasis and 
Urban Transition Residential. The optimal City zoning for the future land use designation of 
Mixed-use Commercial is MUB Mixed-use Business, but commercial or higher-intensity 
residential zoning may also be appropriate. The optimal City zoning for the proposed future land 
use designation of Urban Transition Residential is LR Low-density Residential or RR Rural 
Residential, but slightly higher-intensity or lower-intensity residential zoning may also be 
appropriate, especially on the edges of the area designated. 
 
Based on the future land use designations, the proposed LR zone is the optimal zone for areas 
proposed to be zoned LR. While MUB is the optimal zone for the area designated Mixed-use 
Commercial, the CB zone is an acceptable alternative which would allow commercial 
development at the intersection. The area proposed to be zoned MR serves as a transition 
between the mixed-use area to the south and the lower-intensity residential areas to the north.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
To the north and east, the existing zoning is County LR and designated Urban Transition 
Residential by the comprehensive plan. The area of this re-zoning adjacent to this land is 
proposed to be zoned LR, which is the City zone most analogous to that County zone and 
considered the most appropriate City zone for areas and designated Urban Transition 
Residential.  
 
The portion of this zone change proposed to be zoned MR is adjacent to areas zoned MU and NB 
in the County and designated Mixed-use Commercial, across a major transportation route. The 
MR zone is a lower-intensity zone than these adjacent zones, and would not have an undue 
influence on the surrounding parcels to the south. 
 
The area proposed to be zoned CB is at the intersection of two major transportation routes. The 
property to the south is zoned MU in the County and designated Mixed-use Commercial. The 
property to the west is zoned AG in the City and also designated Mixed-use Commercial. The AG 
zone is a holding zone for future development  the area zoned AG adjacent to the area 
proposed to be zoned CB is anticipated to be zoned commercial use in the near future (likely CB 
zoning). While CB zoning is generally considered a higher-intensity zone than MU, the 
commercial expectation of the future land use designation and the location at a major 
intersection means CB zoning is compatible with nearby property. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current County LR zone allows lower-density, urban residential style uses, and the County 
MU zone allows a variety of commercial and residential uses  but in order to develop with lots 
any smaller than 5 acres, connections to a central sanitary sewer system is required. There is no 

UDC-21-00198
June 7, 2021
sanitary sewer system available in this area of the County outside of the Board of Public Utilities. 
The BOPU generally only services land within City limits (special exceptions are required to 
service unannexed land). The requirement for sanitary sewer or minimum lot sizes for septic 
system provision limits development options. 
 
As the property is proposed to be annexed, City zoning is required. The proposed City LR zone is 
the zone most similar to the current County LR zone, and provides a similar range of 
development options, but by being part of the City, BOPU sewer would be anticipated to be 
extended to the parcel, allowing functional LR-style development. 
 
The MR zone allows a broader mix of residential uses than the current County LR zone. The CB 
zone allows a range of commercial uses, but limits residential development.    
   
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The property is constrained by topography, adjacent major transportation routes, and previous 
development. The ability to create blocks and connections that meet the standards of Article 4 
would be limited, no matter what City zoning was proposed. Also, due to the existing patterns, 
the ability of this land to meet optimal intersection spacing or block arrangement may be 
limited. The property is able to meet other standards of Article 4, but specific application of 
these standards to this land is reviewed and analyzed with the platting action. A concurrent 
Preliminary Plat is proposed with this zone change application.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The CB zone is intended for retail, employment, service and civic uses that may serve a number 
of residential neighborhoods. Typically, areas zoned CB are expected to be more automobile-
oriented and serve the region. It is typically expected in areas designated as activity centers or 
Community Business by the comprehensive plan. The future land use map designates the 
intersection of Christensen and US 30 as an activity center, and the area around the intersection 
of Whitney and US 30 as Community Business. Commercial development at this intersection is 
anticipated by the properties mixed-use future land use designation, and any commercial 
development at this intersection would likely have a regional service area (including the 
surrounding rural and low-to-medium density residential developments). 
 
The LR zone is intended for a mix of lower density residential development with access to public 
infrastructure and city services. LR zoning is anticipated in areas designated Urban Transition 
Residential and should be less than a mile from commercial development. This area is 
designated Urban Transition Residential and is practically adjacent to current and future 
commercial development. 
 
The MR zone is intended for a mix of residential densities and dwelling types with access to 
public infrastructure and city services. MR zoning is anticipated in areas designated Urban 

UDC-21-00198
June 7, 2021
Residential and should be less than a half mile from commercial development. While this area is 
not designated Urban Residential, the area proposed to be zoned MR provides a transition from 
the area zoned LR and nearby commercial or mixed-use zoning. 
 
General commercial design standards apply to the CB zone. The area zoned CB would be 
required to provide a landscape buffer to the adjacent area zoned LR. The area zoned CB is not 
anticipated to have issues meeting the design standards. 
 
Any residential development within the City is expected to meet the general residential design 
standards. These standards apply to the LR and MR zones, and these zone districts have no 
zone-specific standards. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The applicant is seeking the proposed zone change in concert with an annexation. The proposed 
HI and LI zoning does not have zone-specific design standards beyond setback requirements, 
and the parcels proposed to be zoned LI and HI are large enough to allow development to meet 
the setback requirements. The proposed AG zoning is the zone most similar to the existing 
zoning on the property and on surrounding County properties. Development on the property 
zoned AG would require a future rezoning. 
 
A specific development pattern is proposed on the land to be zoned, and the zoning amendment 
is dependent on alignment with the associated plat. Should the specific plat proposed for the 
land not be recorded after the zone change is approved, a separate zoning action should be 
pursued. Should the plat be recorded, but subsequently not developed, the areas zoned LR and 
MR could be replatted to accommodate different development patterns which would still be 
appropriate at this location. 
 
There is no specific development proposed for the parcel to be zoned CB, and the CB-zoned 
parcel would have multiple development options.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-21-00198
June 7, 2021
8)
Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
East Dell Range Boulevard / US 30 Corridor Study 
 
Whitney Road Corridor Plan 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
Both the East Dell Range Boulevard / US 30 Corridor Study and the Whitney Road Corridor Plan both 
anticipate the development of this area with urban-style uses. 
 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Alignment of information shown on the zone change map and preliminary plat map. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The zoning map 
amendment application is proposed in concert with an annexation. The CB, LR, and MR zones are 
appropriate based on the future land use designation for the subject property. Surrounding zoning 
includes lower-density residential and commercial zoning. Surrounding development patterns include 
rural residential and commercial development. The proposed zone change would not have an undue 
influence on the surrounding area. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 

UDC-21-00198
June 7, 2021
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County MU and County LR to CB, LR 
and MR. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Aerial, Future Land Use Plan, and Current Zoning Map 
2. Master Application 
3. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director 
Subject: UDC-17-00623 – Enhanced Use Lease, Annexation  
Date: 
June 28, 2021 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is an Annexation. This is approved by ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral June 28, 2021 
• 
Public Services Committee: July 7, 2021 
• 
Second Reading: July 12, 2021 
• 
Public Services Committee: July 20, 2021 
• 
Third and Final Reading: July 25, 2021 
 
This proposed City-initiated annexation is for approximately 75 acres of land southwest of the I-25 and 
Missile Drive interchange. A portion of these 75 acres historically has been utilized for parking during 
Cheyenne Frontier Days. This land has been designated by F. E. Warren Air Force Base as the “Enhanced 
Use Lease” (EUL) area. The enhanced use Lease program is intended to allow revenue generating special 
projects for the base by utilizing public/private partnerships.   
 
This City-initiated annexation will not require an owner’s signature. To initiate an annexation, the City 
must adopt a Resolution certifying that the annexation complies with Wyoming State Statute 15-1-402. 
After the City adopts this Resolution, the annexation follows the typical schedule, including a public 
hearing and the approval of an Ordinance which annexes the land. This resolution was adopted by the 
Governing Body on December 19, 2019. 
 
As with any annexation, City services will be extended to service the land contained in the annexation. 
City water mains are already located in the vicinity. Sewer mains will need to be extended to the 
property. Police, fire, and sanitation services will be coordinated with F. E. Warren Air Force Base. As the 
land contained in this annexation is vacant, infrastructure improvements required related to the 
annexation are minimal. Future development on the site will require on- and off-site infrastructure 
improvements.  
 
The future land use map of PlanCheyenne designates the area as Military/Federal within the Urban 
Service Boundary. While the plan is silent on recommended uses or development patterns for the 
Military/Federal designation, land within the Urban Service Boundary is expected to develop with urban 
uses and served by urban services.  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
The proposed annexation is adjacent to land under City jurisdiction. Development in this area is a 
natural extension of the City. Approximately 18.2 % of the perimeter of the annexation area is 
contiguous  to City Limits.  Staff notes the purpose of annexation, adjacent highway and railroad rights-
of-way are excluded. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria found in Wyoming Statute 15-1-402, staff recommends approval of 
this annexation. 
 
The Planning Commission considered establishment of Support Services (SS) District at their June 21, 
2021 meeting and recommended the Governing Body establish SS District zoning for the area.  The 
related zoning ordinance is a companion item on this agenda.      
 
Location Map: 
 
 
 
Staff Recommendation: 
Staff recommends the Governing Body approve the Annexation Ordinance.  
 
Attachments: 
1. Ordinance  
Annexation Area

Subject: UDC-21-00219 – Enhanced Use Lease, Zoning  
Date: 
June 28, 2021 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Rezoning. This is approved by ordinance.  
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral: June 28, 2021 
• 
Public Services Committee: July 7, 2021 
• 
Second Reading: July 12, 2021 
• 
Public Services Committee: July 20, 2021 
• 
Third and Final Reading: July 25, 2021 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County X (Exempt) to SS (Support Services) 
zoning district for the area commonly referred to as the ‘Enhanced Use  Lease Area (EUL).’ The area is 
proposed to be annexed to the City of Cheyenne and therefore requires a City zone. The EUL is anticipated to 
be open to the public and to include a mix of commercial, office, and multifamily development.  A related 
annexation ordinance is a companion item on this agenda.          
  
The Planning Commission reviewed this item at their June 21, 2021, meeting and recommends the 
Governing Body approve the rezoning.  The Planning Commission staff report is attached and is 
unchanged.

2. Planning Commission Staff Report

UDC-21-00219  
 
 
June 21, 2021 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of 75.277 acres of land from County X (Exempt) zone district to SS 
(Support Services) zone district, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County X (Exempt) to SS 
(Support Services) zoning district for the area commonly referred to as the ‘Enhanced Use 
Lease Area (EUL).’ The area is proposed to be annexed to the City of Cheyenne and 
therefore requires a City zone. The EUL is anticipated to be open to the public and to 
include a mix of commercial, office, and multifamily development.   
 
A UDC text amendment creating the SS zoning district was reviewed by the Planning 
Commission at their October 5, 2020 Planning Commission meeting and was approved by 
the Governing Body at their November 12th meeting.  The purpose of the new zone district 
was to a create a new district that recognizes needed flexibility for government owned 
lands associated with Francis E. Warren Air Force Base that may develop as civilian-
accessible property within the City limits.  The standard zone districts in the City do not 
necessarily contemplate the unique land ownership structure (leases and other 
arrangements) that may accompany development. 
 
On February 3, 2020 the Planning Commission reviewed a proposed zone change to MUB 
(Mixed Use Business) zoning district and recommended the Governing Body approve the  
change.  At the request of the landowner, this was not forwarded onto the Governing Body 
for consideration.   
 
On November 2, 2020 the Planning Commission accepted withdrawal of a proposed change  
UDC-20-00443.
